Mathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic

LinearEquiv.congrQuadraticMapdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the congruence quadratic map of e (from the name)
English
Let $e$ be a linear equivalence of $N$ and $P$. Then the congruence quadratic map of $e$ is a linear equivalence of the set of quadratic maps of $M$ and $N$ and the set of quadratic maps of $M$ and $P$.
Formula
$e \in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (R), N , P)\vdash \operatorname{congrQuadraticMap} (e)\in \mathrm{LinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (R), \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N), \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , P))$
LinearMap.BilinMap.toQuadraticMapdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the quadratic map of B (from the name)
English
Let $B$ be a bilinear map of $M$ and $N$. Then the quadratic map of $B$ is a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$.
Formula
$B \in \operatorname{BilinMap} (R , M , N)\vdash \operatorname{toQuadraticMap} (B)\in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N)$
LinearMap.BilinMap.toQuadraticMapAddMonoidHomdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the quadratic map additive monoid homomorphism of R and M (from the name)
English
Let $R$ and $M$ be sets. Then the quadratic map additive monoid homomorphism of $R$ and $M$ is an additive monoid homomorphism of the set of bilinear maps of $M$ and $N$ and the set of quadratic maps of $M$ and $N$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $\operatorname{toQuadraticMap}$ and $\mathrm{LinearMap\_\_BilinMap\_\_toQuadraticMap\_zero}$ applied to $R$ and $M$ and $\mathrm{LinearMap\_\_BilinMap\_\_toQuadraticMap\_add}$.
Formula
$R , M \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{toQuadraticMapAddMonoidHom} (R , M):= \operatorname{mk} (\operatorname{mk} (\operatorname{toQuadraticMap} , \mathrm{LinearMap\_\_BilinMap\_\_toQuadraticMap\_zero} (R , M)), \mathrm{LinearMap\_\_BilinMap\_\_toQuadraticMap\_add})\in \mathrm{AddMonoidHom} (\operatorname{BilinMap} (R , M , N), \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N))$
LinearMap.BilinMap.toQuadraticMapLinearMapdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the quadratic map linear map of R and M (from the name)
English
Let $S$, $R$ and $M$ be sets. Then the quadratic map linear map of $R$ and $M$ is a linear map of the set of bilinear maps of $M$ and $N$ and the set of quadratic maps of $M$ and $N$.
Formula
$S , R , M \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{toQuadraticMapLinearMap} (S , R , M)\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (S), \operatorname{BilinMap} (R , M , N), \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N))$
LinearMap.compQuadraticMapdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the composition quadratic map of f and Q (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a linear map of $N$ and $P$. Let $Q$ be a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$. Then the composition quadratic map of $f$ and $Q$ is a quadratic map of $M$ and $P$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), N , P), Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N)\vdash \operatorname{compQuadraticMap} (f , Q)\in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , P)$
LinearMap.compQuadraticMap'defMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the composition quadratic map of f and Q (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a linear map of $N$ and $P$. Let $Q$ be a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$. Then the composition quadratic map of $f$ and $Q$ is a quadratic map of $M$ and $P$ defined as the composition quadratic map of $f$ and the scalar restriction of $Q$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (S), N , P), Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N)\vdash \operatorname{compQuadraticMap'} (f , Q):= \operatorname{compQuadraticMap} (f , \operatorname{restrictScalars} (Q))\in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (S , M , P)$
Matrix.toQuadraticForm'defMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the quadratic form of M (from the name)
English
Let $M$ be a matrix of $n$ and $R$. Then the quadratic form of $M$ is a quadratic form of $R$ and the set of functions from elements of $n$ to elements of $R$ defined as the quadratic map of $\mathrm{Matrix\_\_toLinearMap\_u2082\_'\_\_app}$ applied to $R$ and $M$.
Formula
$M \in \mathrm{Matrix} (n , n , R)\vdash \operatorname{toQuadraticForm'} (M):= \operatorname{toQuadraticMap} (\mathrm{Matrix\_\_toLinearMap\_u2082\_'\_\_app} (R , M))\in \mathrm{QuadraticForm} (R , n \rightarrow R)$
QuadraticFormdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, type
Wording
quadratic form of R and M (from the name)
English
Let $R$ and $M$ be sets. Then a quadratic form of $R$ and $M$ is an element of the function that maps $R$ and $M$ to the set of quadratic maps of $M$ and $R$.
Formula
$R , M \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{QuadraticForm} (R , M):= \lambda R , M . \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , R)$
QuadraticForm.discrdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the discriminant of b and Q (from the docstring)
English
Let $b$ be a basis of $R$ and $N$. Let $Q$ be a quadratic form of $R$ and $N$. Then the discriminant of $b$ and $Q$ is an element of $R$ defined as the determinant of the matrix of $b$ and $Q$.
Formula
$b \in \operatorname{Basis} (n , R , N), Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticForm} (R , N)\vdash \operatorname{discr} (b , Q):= \operatorname{det} (\operatorname{toMatrix} (b , Q))\in R$

Wording
the discriminant of Q (from the docstring)
English
Let $Q$ be a quadratic form of $R$ and the set of functions from elements of $n$ to elements of $R$. Then the discriminant of $Q$ is an element of $R$ defined as the determinant of the matrix of $Q$.
Formula
$Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticForm} (R , n \rightarrow R)\vdash \operatorname{discr'} (Q):= \operatorname{det} (\operatorname{toMatrix'} (Q))\in R$

QuadraticMapstructureMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, type
Wording
quadratic map of M and N (from the name)
English
Let $R$, $M$ and $N$ be sets. Then the quadratic maps of $M$ and $N$ are a basic type.
Formula
$R , M , N \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N)\ \mathsf{set}$
QuadraticMap.AnisotropicdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, predicate
Wording
Q is anisotropic (from the name)
English
Let $Q$ be a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$. Then $Q$ is anisotropic, if for all elements $x_{5}$ of $M$, if $\EqtMACRoH {Q (x_{5})}{0}$, then $\EqtMACRoH {x_{5}}{0}$.
Formula
$Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N)\vdash \operatorname{Anisotropic} (Q):\Leftrightarrow \forall x_{5} \in M . \EqtMACRoJ {Q (x_{5})}{0}\rightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {x_{5}}{0}$
QuadraticMap.IsOrthodefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, predicate
Wording
x5 is orthogonal to y (from the name, with words of the lexicon)
English
Let $Q$ be a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$. Let $x_{5}$ and $y$ be elements of $M$. Then $x_{5}$ is orthogonal to $y$, if $\EqtMACRoH {Q (x_{5} + y)}{Q (x_{5})+ Q (y)}$.
Formula
$Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N), x_{5} , y \in M \vdash \operatorname{IsOrtho} (Q , x_{5} , y):\Leftrightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {Q (x_{5} + y)}{Q (x_{5})+ Q (y)}$
QuadraticMap.PosDefdefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, predicate
Wording
x7 is a positive part (from the name)
English
Let $x_{7}$ be a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$. Then $x_{7}$ is a positive part, if for all elements $x_{8}$ of $M$, if $x_{8} \neq 0$, then $0 < x_{7} (x_{8})$.
Formula
$x_{7} \in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (x , M , N)\vdash \operatorname{PosDef} (x_{7}):\Leftrightarrow \forall x_{8} \in M . x_{8} \neq 0 \rightarrow 0 < x_{7} (x_{8})$

QuadraticMap.copydefMathlib.LinearAlgebra.QuadraticForm.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the copy of Q and h (from the name)
English
Let $Q$ be a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$. Let $\mathrm{Q'}$ be a function from elements of $M$ to elements of $N$ such that $\mathrm{Q'}$ is equal to $\operatorname{coe}$ applied to $Q$. Then the copy of $Q$ and $\mathrm{Q'}$ is a quadratic map of $M$ and $N$.
Formula
$Q \in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N), \mathrm{Q'} \in M \rightarrow N , \EqtMACRoJ {\mathrm{Q'}}{\operatorname{coe} (Q)}\vdash \operatorname{copy} (Q , \mathrm{Q'} , h)\in \mathrm{QuadraticMap} (R , M , N)$
